Then \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-3 and |c(M_{1})|=n_{1}-1. Furthermore, since |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}-1 we have that c_{2}^{\prime} is 2 -uniform and e\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}). By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S . Thus (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has an S -SRCP \{C_{1},C_{2}\} where we may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in C_{i}. 
Let M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} and extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i}):=0,\ i=1,2. If (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit, say C , then C^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. Assuming s_{1}\in C, then C\triangle C_{1} would be a rainbow cycle in M , a contradiction. Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic and hence M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. Given that \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{1}-1, it follows by assumption that Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and T. 
Suppose Theorem 2 i.1) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Then (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) has an T -SRCT \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} where we may assume that for all i\in[2], if s_{i}\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{3}D_{j} , then s_{i}\in D_{i} . For i=1,2, let D_{i}^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}D_{i}&\mathrm{if}\ s_{i}\not\in D_{i}\\
C_{i}\triangle D_{i}&\mathrm{if}\ s_{i}\in D_{i}.\end{array}\right. Since |C_{1}|+|C_{2}|\leq r(M_{2})+2=n_{2}+2 and for all 1\leq i<j\leq 3 , |D_{i}|+|D_{j}|\leq r(M_{1})+2=n_{1}+2 , it follows that




 \displaystyle|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}| 
 \displaystyle\leq|D_{1}|+|D_{2}|+|C_{1}|+|C_{2}|-4 




 \displaystyle\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+4-4 




 \displaystyle=n+2. 



Furthermore, it is seen that for i=1,2, |D_{i}^{\prime}|+|D_{3}|\leq n+2. 
Thus \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}\} is seen to be a T -SRCyT for (M,c) and Theorem 2 i.1) holds for (M,c). 
If instead Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) , then one can use similar arguments to show that Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M,c) as well.




Case 2.2 (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) is type 2.




We have |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2} and (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) contains exactly two colour-singular elements, say f and g. We also have that \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-3 and (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has one or three colour-singular elements.




Case 2.2.1 e\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}). 




We have that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has exactly three colour-singular elements being e,f^{\prime}, g^{\prime} and |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=n_{1}. Let c_{2}^{\prime\prime} be a colouring of M_{2}^{\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(g):=c_{2}^{\prime}(f) and \forall x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime})-g,\ c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x):=c_{2}^{\prime}(x). 
By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and S and hence there exists an S -SRCP \{C_{1},C_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) where we may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in C_{i}. 
Extend c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} for M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i}),\ i=1,2 are defined as follows. If \{f,g\}\cap(C_{1}\cup C_{2})=\mathchar 31, then define (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})):=(c(f),c(g)). If \{f,g\}\cap(C_{1}\cup C_{2})\neq\mathchar 31 
then define




 (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(c(f),c(g))&\mathrm{if}\ f\in C_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ g\in C_{2}\\
(c(g),c(f))&\mathrm{if}\ g\in C_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ f\in C_{2}\end{array}\right. 



We claim that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. For if it has a rainbow circuit, say D , then D\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. If |D\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}|=1, and say s_{1}\in D, then D\triangle C_{1} is a rainbow cycle of M , a contradiction. If \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset D, then let D^{\prime}=D\triangle S=D-s_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3}. Since |c(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}, M_{2}^{\prime}+s_{3} has an s_{3} -rainbow circuit C where s_{3}\in C. Noting that \{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}\}\cap D^{\prime}=\mathchar 31, it follows that C\triangle D^{\prime} is a rainbow cycle of M , a contradiction. Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic.




By symmetry, we may assume that (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}))=(c(f),c(g)). We may assume that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. For if not, there is an element x\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s_{1} or s_{2}. In this case, redefine M_{1} and M_{2} so that x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) . Now \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1 and we can revert back to Case 0. Thus we may assume that M_{1} is simple.
By assumption, Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . Suppose Theorem 2 i.1) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and T . Then there exists a T -SRCT \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Let D=\bigcup_{i}D_{i}. If \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\cap D=\mathchar 31 , then \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} is seen to be a T -SRCT for (M,c) .
Suppose \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\cap D\neq\mathchar 31. If |\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\cap D|=1 and say, s_{1}\in D_{1} , then let D_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}\triangle D_{1}. Now \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2},D_{3}\} is seen to be a T -SRCyT for (M,c). Suppose |\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\cap D|=2. Suppose two of the circuits D_{i},\ i=1,2,3 intersect \{s_{1},s_{2}\}. Then we may assume that s_{i}\in D_{i},\ i=1,2. Let D_{i}^{\prime}=C_{i}\triangle D_{i},\ i=1,2. Given that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is an S -SRCP for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) ,
we have




 \displaystyle|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}| 
 \displaystyle=|D_{1}|+|D_{2}|+|C_{1}|+|C_{2}|-4 




 \displaystyle\leq n_{1}+2+n_{2}+2-4 




 \displaystyle=n+2. 



Furthermore, it is seen that for i=1,2, |D_{i}^{\prime}|+|D_{3}|\leq n+2. 
Thus \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}\} is a T -SRCyT for (M,c). Suppose instead that for some i, \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subseteq D_{i}. We may assume that
this is true for i=1. 
Similar to before, there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. Let D_{1}^{\prime}=D_{1}\triangle S\triangle C. We observe that \{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}\}\cap D_{1}^{\prime}=\mathchar 31 and thus D_{1}^{\prime} is a rainbow cycle in (M,c). 
It is now seen that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2},D_{3}\} is a T -SRCyT for (M,c). 
It follows from the above that if Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}), then Theorem 2 i.1) holds for (M,c). 
If instead Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}), then using the similar arguments as above, one can show that Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M,c) as well.




Case 2.2.2 e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}). 




We have that (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has exactly two colour-singular elements, one which is e and the other which we denote by f. It follows that |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2} and f^{\prime} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
Let c_{2}^{\prime\prime} be a colouring of M_{2}^{\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f):=c(e) and for all x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime})-f,\ c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x):=c(x). 
By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and S hence there is an S -SRCP \{C_{1},C_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) where for i=1,2, s_{i}\in C_{i}. 
Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}+s_{1}+s_{2} where (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}))=(c(e),c(f)), if \{e,f\}\cap(C_{1}\cup C_{2})=\mathchar 31 ; otherwise,




 (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(c(e),c(f))&\mathrm{if}\ e\in C_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ f\in C_{2}\\
(c(f),c(e))&\mathrm{if}\ f\in C_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ e\in C_{2}\end{array}\right. 



Suppose (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) has a rainbow circuit, say D . Then D\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. If for some i\in[2], D\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\{s_{i}\}, then D\triangle C_{i} is seen to be a rainbow cycle in M . If \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subseteq D, then D^{\prime}=D\triangle S=D-s_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3} is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f^{\prime}\not\in D^{\prime}. 
Since |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}, it follows that there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit C^{\prime} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and one sees that C^{\prime}\triangle D^{\prime} is a rainbow cycle in M , a contradiction. We conclude that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic.
As before, we may assume that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple; stronger still, we may assume that no element of E(M_{1}^{\prime}) is parallel with any element of S. 
By assumption,Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Suppose that Theorem 2 i.1) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and let \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} be a T -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). We may assume that \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\cap\bigcup_{i}D_{i}\neq\mathchar 31. 
Suppose that for some i\in[3],\ \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset D_{i}. We may assume that \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset D_{1}. Let C be a s_{3} -rainbow circuit in M_{2}^{\prime}+f^{\prime} where |C|\leq n_{2}. 
Let D_{1}^{\prime}=D_{1}\triangle S\triangle C . Then \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2},D_{3}\} is seen to be a T -SRCyT for (M,c). 
Suppose that for all i\in[3], |D_{i}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}|\leq 1. We may assume that for all i\in[2], if s_{i}\in\bigcup_{i}D_{i}, then s_{i}\in D_{i}. For i=1,2 , define D_{i}^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D_{i}\triangle C_{i}&\mathrm{if}\ s_{i}\in D_{i}\\
D_{i}&\mathrm{if}s_{i}\not\in D_{i}.\end{array}\right. Then \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}\} is seen to be a T -SRCyT for (M,c). 




Suppose that Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and T. For convenience, we may assume that c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1})=c(e) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})=c(f). Then s_{1} is the unique colour-singular element in (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). 
Let x\in T and let \{D_{1},D_{2}\} be a T -SRCP in (M_{1}^{\prime\prime}-s_{1},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) where x\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}. Now using the circuits C_{2} and D_{i},\ i\in[2], it is straightforward to construct a T -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M-e,c) where x\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}. Thus Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M,c). 






10 The proof of Theorem 2 ii)




As in the previous section, let M be a simple regular matroid where \varepsilon(M)=2n-1 and let c be a 2 -bounded n -colouring of M where (M,c) is rainbow circuit-free.
Let e be the unique singular element of (M,c). Let N be a regular extension of M by and element x where e and x are non-parallel.
As before, we may assume that N is connected and is neither graphic nor cographic. Note that M\not\simeq M(K_{3,3}) since any 2 -bounded 5 -colouring of M(K_{3,3}) must contain a rainbow circuit. As such N\not\simeq R_{10} since deleting an element from R_{10} results in a matroid isomorphic to M(K_{3,3}). Thus N is either a 2 -sum or 3 -sum of regular matroids.




10.1 The case where N=N_{1}\oplus_{2}N_{2} 




Assume that N is a 2 -sum of regular matroids, N=N_{1}\oplus_{2}N_{2} where x\in E(N_{1}) and E(N_{1})\cap E(N_{2})=\{s\}. 
Let M_{1}=N_{1}\backslash x and M_{2}=N_{2}. For i=1,2, let n_{i}=r(M_{i}), M_{i}^{\prime}=M_{i}\backslash s, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=\varepsilon(M_{i}^{\prime}) and let c_{i}^{\prime}=c\big|E(M_{i}^{\prime}). We have that n=n_{1}+n_{2}-1. and \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)-1. Thus for some i, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\geq 2n_{i}-1. On the other hand, since for i=1,2, M_{i}^{\prime} is circuit-achromatic, it follows that \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{i}-1. Thus for some i, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=2n_{i}-1 and \varepsilon_{3-i}^{\prime}=2n_{3-i}-2. 




Case 1 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-1. 




We have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=n_{2}-2 and (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) contains a unique colour-singular element say f. 
Suppose x and f are non-parallel. By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) and as such there exists an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Now \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is also seen to be a x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Thus we may assume that x and f are parallel. Then f\neq e and hence e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}), 
 |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}, and e and f^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Let C_{1}=\{x,f\}. 
There is a s -rainbow circuit, say C, for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. Since |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=n_{1}, there is a rainbow-subset B\subset E(M_{1}^{\prime})-f where |B|=n_{1}-1. Now B+x+s contains a circuit D . If x\not\in D, then C\triangle D is seen to be a rainbow cycle of (M,c). Thus x\in D. If s\not\in D, then
 \{C_{1},D\} is seen to be a x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). If instead s\in D, then \{C_{1},C\triangle D\} is seen to be a x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 2 \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1 




We may assume that x is not parallel to s ; otherwise, we may assume that x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}), in which case we can apply the previous case with the roles of M_{1}^{\prime} and M_{2}^{\prime} reversed.
We observe that M_{1} is simple; for otherwise, if some element y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) is parallel to s , then M_{2}^{\prime}+y would be a simple matroid with 2n_{2} elements, and hence it would contain a rainbow circuit, a contradiction.
We may also assume that M_{2} is simple. For if there is an element y\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s , then we can assume that y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) and revert back to Case 1.
Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1} of M_{1} where c_{1}(s):=0. 




Case 2.1 e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}). 




We have that e is the unique singular element of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and thus s is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1},c_{1}) (which is not parallel with x ). Since |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}, there is an s -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). 
Now (M_{1},c_{1}) is seen to be circuit-achromatic. For if it contained a rainbow circuit, say D , then s\in D and C\triangle D is a rainbow circuit in (M,c) , a contradiction. Thus (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic and Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1},c_{1}). As such
there exists an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1},c_{1}). If s\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}, then \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Suppose s\in D_{1}\cup D_{2} , where we may assume that s\in D_{1}. Let D_{1}^{\prime}=C\triangle D_{1}. Then it is seen that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 2.2 e\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}). 




Let f be the unique colour-singular element of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) . Then e and f^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1} of M_{1} where c_{1}(s):=c(f). There is an s -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. If (M_{1},c_{1}) contains a rainbow circuit, say D , then s\in D (and hence f^{\prime}\not\in D ) and C\triangle D is seen to be a rainbow circuit of (M,c). Thus (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1},c_{1}) and hence there exists an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1},c_{1}) where we may assume that s\in D_{1}\cup D_{2} (otherwise \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c) ). In particular, we may assume that s\in D_{1}. Let D_{1}^{\prime}=C\triangle D_{1}. Then \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}\} is seen to be an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 





10.2 The case where N=N_{1}\oplus_{3}N_{2} 




Assume that N is a 3 -sum of regular matroids, N=N_{1}\oplus_{3}N_{2} where E(N_{1})\cap E(N_{2})=S=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\} and x\in E(N_{1}). Let M_{1}=N_{1}\backslash x and M_{2}=N_{2}. For i=1,2, let n_{i}=r(M_{i}), M_{i}^{\prime}=M_{i}\backslash S, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=\varepsilon(M_{i}^{\prime}), and let c_{i}^{\prime}=c\big|E(M_{i}^{\prime}). We have that n=n_{1}+n_{2}-2 and \varepsilon(M)=2n-1=2(n_{1}+n_{2}-2)-1 and thus for some i\in\{1,2\}, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\geq 2n_{i}-2. As before, we have that for i=1,2, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{i}-1. 
We shall examine a number of cases.




Case 1: For some i\in[2], \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=2n_{i}-1. 




Case 1.1 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-1 




We have \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-4. Let f be the unique singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Suppose x and f are parallel. Then f\neq e and hence e and f^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). 
Then |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}-1 and it follows by Lemma 9.2 that for some j\in[3] , there exists an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. We may assume this is true for j=1. Let C_{1}=\{x,f\}. 
Since |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=n_{1}, there is a rainbow subset B\subset E(M_{1}^{\prime})-f where |B|=n_{1}-1. Then B+x+s_{1} contains a circuit D. Clearly x\in D for otherwise, s_{1}\in D and C\triangle D is a rainbow cycle. If s_{1}\not\in D, then \{C_{1},D\} is seen to be a
 x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Otherwise, if s_{1}\in D, then \{C_{1},C\triangle D\} is a x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c). 




Suppose that x and f are non-parallel. Then by assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) and hence there is an x -semi-SRCP for
 (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) which is also seen to be an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2: \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1. 




We have \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-4. We may assume that x is not parallel with any element of S , for otherwise, we may just assume that x\in E(N_{2}) and revert back to Case 1.1, with the roles of M_{1} and M_{2} interchanged. Let f be the unique colour-singular element of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). By assumption, Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and thus for all 1\leq i<j\leq 3 , there exists an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where
 s_{i}\in D_{1} and s_{j}\in D_{2}. We also note that M_{1} must be simple. For if not, then there is an element y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel to an element of S . As such, M_{2}^{\prime}+y is a simple matroid having 2n_{2} elements and thus has a rainbow circuit,
a contradiction. Thus M_{1} is simple.




Case 1.2.1: f=e. 




Assume that if e is parallel with an element of S , then it is parallel with s_{3}. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1} of M_{1} where for i=1,2, c_{1}(s_{i}):=a and c_{1}(s_{3}):=b (and a,b\not\in c(M) ). Suppose that (M_{1},c_{1}) contains a rainbow circuit C . Then
 C\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. Suppose for some i , C\cap S=\{s_{i}\}. Then given that there is an s_{i} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}), C\triangle D is a rainbow cycle of (M,c) , a contradiction. Suppose |C\cap S|=2. We may assume that
 C\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. Given that there is an s_{2} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}), it is seen that C\triangle S\triangle D is a rainbow cycle for (M,c). It follows that (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic.




We observe that s_{3} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1},c_{1}). By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1},c_{1}) . Since x and s_{3} are non-parallel, there is an x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1},c_{1}). If S\cap(C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime})=\mathchar 31, then \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for
 (M,c). Suppose that for some i , C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31 and C_{3-i}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31. We may assume this is true for i=1 and s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Suppose C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\}. Let D be an s_{1} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{2}. 
Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D, we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x - semi-SRCyP for (M,c). Suppose instead that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. Then C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. Let D be an s_{2} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x - semi-SRCyP for (M,c). 




Finally, suppose that for i=1,2 , C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. Assume first that for i=1,2, |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=1 . We may assume that C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i}\},\ i=1,2. Let \{D_{1},D_{2}\} be a S -SRCP for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where s_{i}\in D_{i},\ i=1,2. 
For i=1,2, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}. Then \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is seen to be an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Assume now that for some i , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. We may assume that \{s_{1},s_{3}\}\subset C_{1}^{\prime} and s_{2}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. 
By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and (noting that e and s_{2} are non-parallel) there is an s_{2} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2} , we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2.2: f\neq e. 




We have that e and f^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). We largely repeat our arguments from the previous case, with some modifications. As noted above, Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S .
If i.1) holds, then there is an S -SRCT \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). In this case, we may assume that if for some i , f\in D_{i} , then s_{1}\in D_{i}. Notably, regardless of whether Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) , for i=2,3 , there is a S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where s_{2}\in D_{1},\ s_{3}\in D_{2} and f\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}. In particular, this implies that for all i\in\{2,3\}, there is an
 s_{i} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where f\not\in D and |D|\leq n_{2}. 




Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1} of M_{1} where c_{1}(s_{1}):=c(f) and c_{1}(s_{i}):=0,\ i=2,3. Then e is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1},c_{1}) . Using similar arguments as in the previous case, one can show that (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic. Given that M_{1} is simple, it follows
by assumption that Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1},c_{1}) and consequently there is an x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1},c_{1}). We may assume that S\cap(C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime})\neq\mathchar 31 , for otherwise, \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2.2.1 For some i , C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31 and C_{3-i}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31. 




We may assume that this is true for i=1. Suppose first that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=1. If C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\}, the let D be an s_{1} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{2}. 
Then one sees that for C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D, \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Suppose instead that for some j\in\{2,3\},\ C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}. We may assume s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. 
Let D be an s_{2} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where f\not\in D and |D|\leq n_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c). 
Finally, suppose |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{2}\}. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) for which f\not\in D and |D|\leq n_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D , we see that
 \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2.2.2 For i=1,2, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




Suppose first that s_{1}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime} and s_{3}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. As previously noted, there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where s_{2}\in D_{1},s_{3}\in D_{2}, and f\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}. 
Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i+1},\ i=1,2, we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c). 
Thus we may assume that s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime} and s_{2}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. Suppose that s_{3}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. By assumption, there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where s_{i}\in D_{i},\ i=1,2 and if f\in D_{1}\cup D_{2} , then f\in D_{1}. 
Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i},\ i=1,2 , we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c). Suppose instead that s_{3}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. If f is parallel with s_{2} , then given that there is an s_{2} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where f\not\in D, it would follow that D-s_{2}+f is a rainbow circuit. Thus f and s_{2} are non-parallel.
By assumption, there exists an s_{2} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where we may assume that f\not\in D_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2} , we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCyP in (M,c). 




Case 2: For some i\in[2], \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=2n_{i}-2. 




Case 2.1 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-2 




We have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-3. By Claim 9.2, there is a j\in[3] for which there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. We may assume this is true for j=1 ; that is, s_{1}\in C. 




Case 2.1.1 (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is type 1.




We have that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has no colour-singular elements. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=0. If (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit, say D ,
then s_{1}\in D and hence C\triangle D is a rainbow circuit of (M,c) . Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic and hence M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is also simple. We see that s_{1} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . Suppose first that x and s_{1} are non-parallel. Then there exists an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). If s_{1}\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}, then \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is seen to be an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Thus we may assume that s_{1}\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}, and in particular, s_{1}\in D_{1}. Let D_{1}^{\prime}=C\triangle D_{1}. Then \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}\} is seen to be an x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c) .
Suppose now that x and s_{1} are parallel.
We have that e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) and e and s_{1} are non-parallel. If for some j\in\{2,3\} there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit, then we could use this in place of C in the previous argument, noting that x is not parallel to s_{j}. 
Thus we may assume that for j\in\{2,3\}, there is no s_{j} -rainbow circuit in (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Because of this, M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+s_{2}+s_{3} is simple. Extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2}^{\prime\prime} of M_{2}^{\prime\prime} where for j=2,3, c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{j}):=0. 
Now (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is a simple, coloured matroid with 2n_{2}-1 elements which is also circuit-achromatic.
Given that x is parallel with s_{1} , M_{2}^{\prime\prime}+x is a regular extension of M_{2}^{\prime\prime} and hence Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). Thus there
exists a x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). If \{s_{2},s_{3}\}\cap(C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime})=\mathchar 31, then \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Suppose s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime} , where we may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Then
 C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle\{x,s_{1}\}\triangle S=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}-x-s_{2}+s_{3} is seen to be a s_{3} -rainbow circuit in (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) , contradicting our assumptions. A similar argument can be used if s_{3}\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. 
It follows from the above that (M,c) has an x -semi-SRCP.




Case 2.1.2 (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is type 2.




We have that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) contains two singular elements, say f and g. 




Case 2.1.2.1: e\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}). 




We may assume that g=e. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(f). As in Case 1.2.1, (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Note that e is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). 
Suppose first that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. Then by assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and thus there is an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). We may assume s_{1}\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}, (for otherwise, \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c) ) and in particular, s_{1}\in D_{1}. Let D_{1}^{\prime}=C\triangle D_{1}. Then it is seen that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCyP for (M,c) .




Suppose instead that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is not simple. Then there is an element y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s_{1} . If y\neq f, then C^{\prime}=C-s_{1}+y is seen to be a rainbow circuit of (M,c) , a contradiction. Thus y=f (and f^{\prime}\in C ). Let M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+f and extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2}^{\prime\prime} for
 M_{2}^{\prime\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f):=c(f). Then M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is simple, \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{2}-2 , and (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and thus there exists an S -SRCP \{C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}\}. 
We may assume that s_{2}\in C^{\prime} If f\not\in C^{\prime} , then we can use C^{\prime} in place of C in the previous arguments (noting that f is not parallel with s_{2} ). Thus we may assume f\in C^{\prime} and hence f^{\prime}\not\in C^{\prime}. 
However, D^{\prime}=C^{\prime}\triangle\{f,s_{1}\}\triangle S=C^{\prime}-f-s_{2}+s_{3} is seen to be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and we can use D^{\prime} in place of C in the previous arguments. It follows from the above that (M,c) has an x -semi-SRCP.




Case 2.1.2.2: e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}). 




We see that e , f^{\prime} and g^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}. At least one of f or g is not parallel with x , and we may assume that g is such.
By Lemma 9.2, there exists j\in[3] such that there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where
 g^{\prime}\not\in C and |C|\leq n_{2}. We may assume that this is true j=1; that is, s_{1}\in C. Let M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} and extend c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(f). 
If (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) has a rainbow circuit, say D , then s_{1}\in D and C\triangle D is a rainbow circuit of (M,c), a contradiction (here we use the fact that g^{\prime}\not\in C ). Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Suppose M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. Then by assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) where we note that g is the unique
colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) (and x and g are non-parallel). Thus there an x -semi-SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . We may assume s_{1}\in D_{1}; for if s_{1}\not\in D_{1}\cup D_{2} , then \{D_{1},D_{2}\} is an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). Let D_{1}^{\prime}=C\triangle D_{1}. Then \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}\} is seen to be an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Suppose instead that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is not simple. Then there is an element y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s_{1}. 
Since (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic, it must be that y=f. 
Suppose x and f are parallel. Let C_{1}=\{x,f\} and let
 C_{2}=C\triangle\{f,s_{1}\}\triangle\{x,f\}=C-s_{1}+x. Then \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is seen to be a x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 
Suppose that x is not parallel with f. Then one could argue with the roles of f and g interchanged, in which case we can assume g is parallel with an element of S as well. In this case, we can assume that \{f,g\}\subset E(M_{2}^{\prime}) and \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1. 
We can now revert back to the proof in Case 1.




Case 2.2: \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-2. 




We have that \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-3. We may assume that M_{1} is simple, for if not, there is an element y\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel to an element of S. In this case, we may just assume that y\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}), in which case \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1 and we may revert back to the proof of Case 1.




Case 2.2.1: (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) is type 1.




We have that (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has no colour-singular elements and thus e is the unique colour-singular element for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S. Thus there an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for
 (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) . We may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in D_{i}. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i}):=0,\ i=1,2. 
Seeing that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic, Theorem 2 ii)
holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and x. Thus there is an x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Following similar arguments as before using the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i},\ i=1,2 , one can construct an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 2.2.2: (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) is type 2.




We have that (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has two colour-singular elements which we denote by f and g. 




Case 2.2.2.1: e\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}). 




We may assume that g=e. Then f^{\prime} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Let c_{2}^{\prime\prime} be the colouring obtained from c_{2}^{\prime} by recolouring f so that c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f):=c(e). Then Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and S and there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) .
We may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in D_{i} . Furthermore, we may assume that if f\in D_{1}\cup D_{2}, then f\in D_{1}. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(f), c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}):=0, noting that s_{2} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). 
It is straightforward to show that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Suppose that x and s_{2} are non-parallel. Then applying Theorem 2 ii) to (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}), there is an x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Following similar arguments as before using
the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i},\ i=1,2 , one can construct an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). If x and s_{2} are parallel, then we may assume x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) and we can argue as in Case 1.2.2.1 with the roles of M_{1}^{\prime} and M_{2}^{\prime} switched.




Case 2.2.2.2: e\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}). 




We have that e,f^{\prime},g^{\prime} are the colour-singular elements of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Let c_{2}^{\prime\prime} be the colouring obtained from c_{2}^{\prime} by recolouring g so that c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(g):=c(f). 
As in Case 2.2.2.1, there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) where we may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in D_{i}. 
Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} where




 (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(c(f),c(g))&\mathrm{if}\ f\in D_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ g\in D_{2}\\
(c(g),c(f))&\mathrm{otherwise}\end{array}\right. 



Then e is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and it is straightforward to show that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption, there is an x -semi-SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . Following similar arguments as before using the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i},\ i=1,2, one can construct
an x -semi-SRCP for (M,c). 






11 The proof of Theorem 2 iii)




To begin with, we shall need the following lemma:









11.1 Lemma




Let N be a simple, regular matroid where \varepsilon(N)=2r(N)-3 and let N^{\prime} be a regular extension of N by a set S=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\} which is a 3 -circuit of N^{\prime}. Let c be a 2 -bounded colouring of N where (N,c) is circuit-achromatic. Then (N,c) has one or three colour-singular elements and

i) 



for some j\in[3] there exists an s_{j} -rainbow circuit for (N,c), and

ii) 



if (N,c) has three colour-singular elements
 f_{i},\ i=1,2,3 and N^{\prime}\big|S\cup\{f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\}\not\simeq M(K_{4}), 
then for some j\in[3] there is an s_{j} - rainbow circuit C for (N,c) 
where |C\cap\{f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\}|\leq 1. Moreover, C can be chosen so that |C|\leq r(N)-1. 





Proof.



Since (N,c) is circuit-achromatic, it follows that |c(N)|\leq r(N). Thus if (N,c) has \xi colour-singular elements then \xi is odd (since \varepsilon(N)=2r(N)-3 is odd) and |c(N)|=\frac{2r(N)-3-\xi}{2}+\xi\leq r(N) and hence \xi\leq 3. 
The proof of i) follows from Lemma 9.2.
We shall prove ii) by induction on r(N). When r(N)=3, ii) is seen to be true. Suppose r(N)=n>3, and ii) is true for all regular matroids of rank at most n-1. Assume that (N,c) has three colour-singular elements f_{i},\ i=1,2,3 and let
 F=\{f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\}. Assume that N^{\prime}\big|F\cup S\not\simeq M(K_{4}). We may also assume that for all j\in[3] and for all e\in E(N) there is no parallel class \{e,s_{j}\}. For if such existed, then C=\{e,s_{j}\} would be the desired circuit.
By Theorem 3, c is stratified and we let X_{1},\dots,X_{n} be a stratification. We may assume that for i=1,2,3, X_{i}=\{f_{i}\}. Observe that X=X_{n} is a cocircuit of N and |X|=2. 
Let X=\{e,f\}. Let P=N/e\backslash f, P^{\prime}=N^{\prime}/e\backslash f, and let c^{\prime}=c\big|P. Then (P,c^{\prime}) is simple, circuit-achromatic and S is a 3 -circuit of P^{\prime} and P^{\prime} is a regular extension of P. If P^{\prime}\big|F\cup S\not\simeq M(K_{4}), then by assumption, for some j\in[3], 
there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C^{\prime} for (P,c^{\prime}) for which
 |C^{\prime}\cap F|\leq 1 and |C^{\prime}|\leq r(P)-1=r(N)-2. Now it seen that either C^{\prime} or C=C^{\prime}+e is a circuit in M^{\prime}. If the former holds, then C=C^{\prime} is the desired circuit; otherwise, C=C^{\prime}+e is the desired circuit. As such, we may assume that P^{\prime}\big|F\cup S\simeq M(K_{4}) . The same applies if we switch the roles of e and f ; that is, we may also assume that for Q^{\prime}=N^{\prime}/f\backslash e , Q^{\prime}\big|F\cup S\simeq M(K_{4}). Let N^{\prime\prime}=M^{\prime}\big|F\cup S\cup X. Since N^{\prime\prime} is regular and \varepsilon(N^{\prime\prime})=8 , N^{\prime\prime} is graphic. Let G be a graph where N^{\prime\prime}=M(G). By assumption G/e\backslash f\simeq K_{4}\simeq G/f\backslash e. Given that S corresponds to a triangle and the subgraph of G induced by the edges of F\cup S is not isomorphic to K_{4} , it is straightforward to show that the edges e and f must be parallel in G . This contradicts our assumption that M is simple. Thus for some s_{j}\in S, there is a s_{j} -rainbow circuit C where |C\cap F|\leq 1 and |C|\leq r(N)-1. 
∎





To prove Theorem 2 iii), we shall use induction on r(M) where we assume Theorem 2 is true when r(M)<n. 
Let M be a simple regular matroid where r(M)=n and \varepsilon(M)=2n-2. Let c be a 2 -bounded colouring of M for which (M,c) is circuit-achromatic. We may assume that |c(M)|=n-1 (i.e. c is 2 -uniform). For if there are two colour-singular elements in (M,c), then these can be lumped into one colour class. Let N=M+T be a regular extension of M where T is a 3 -circuit of N which is co-simple.
By the results of Sections 7 and 8, Theorem 2 iii) is true for both graphic and cographic matroids. Because of this
we may assume (as before) that N is a 2 - or 3 - sum of two regular matroids. We shall only deal with the case where N is a 3 -sum as the 2 -sum case is simpler and uses similar arguments.




Let N=N_{1}\oplus_{3}N_{2} where E(N_{1})\cap E(N_{2})=S=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\}. For i=1,2, let (M_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime}),\ n_{i} and \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime} be defined as before. We may assume that T\subseteq E(N_{1}). We have




 2r(M)-2=2(n_{1}+n_{2}-2)-2=2n-6=\varepsilon(M)=\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}. 



We observe that if for some i\in[2],\ \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\geq 2n_{i}-3, then n_{i}-1\leq|c_{i}^{\prime}(M_{i}^{\prime})|\leq n_{i}. In this case, we say (as before) that M_{i}^{\prime} has type 1 if |c_{i}^{\prime}(M_{i}^{\prime})|=n_{i}-1 and has type 2 if |c_{i}^{\prime}(M_{i})|=n_{1}. 
Lastly, we may assume that \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{1}-3 , for otherwise it follows by our assumptions that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a T -SRCP.




Case 1 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-3. 




We have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-3 .




Case 1.1 M_{1}^{\prime} is type 1.




We have that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a unique colour-singular element which is denoted by e. Consequently, e^{\prime} is the unique colour-singular element of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and hence |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}-1. 
By Lemma 9.2, there exists j\in[3] for which there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C for
 (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C|\leq n_{2}. We claim that we may choose C so that M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{j} is simple. For suppose C is an s_{1} -rainbow circuit where M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} is not simple. Then there is an element x\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) which is parallel to s_{1}. Clearly x=e , for otherwise, C-s_{1}+x is a rainbow circuit of (M,c). We have for i=2,3, e and s_{i} are non-parallel and hence M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{i} is simple. Let M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+e and extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring
 c_{2}^{\prime\prime} of M_{2}^{\prime\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(e):=c(e). Then (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is simple, circuit-achromatic and \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{2}-2. By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and S .
Thus there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). For some i , s_{1}\not\in D_{i} , and we may assume that s_{1}\not\in D_{1} and s_{2}\in D_{1}. If e\not\in D_{1}, then D_{1} is the desired circuit, since M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{2} is simple.
If e\in D_{1} , then D_{1}\triangle\{e,s_{1}\}\triangle S=D_{1}-e-s_{2}+s_{3} is seen to be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) (where |D_{1}|\leq n_{2} ) and M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{3} is simple. This proves our claim. Thus we may assume that C is an s_{1} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} is simple.




Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} for M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(e). Now M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple and (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is seen to be circuit-achromatic.
By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and T . Thus there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). We may assume that s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime} , for if s_{1}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}, then
 \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCP for (M,c). Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle C we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2 M_{1}^{\prime} has type 2.




We have that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has three colour-singular elements which we denote by f_{i},\ i=1,2,3, Correspondingly, |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2} and f_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2,3 are the colour-singular elements of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Let F=\{f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\} and F^{\prime}=\{f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime},f_{3}^{\prime}\}. 
We observe that for all i\in[3] and j\in[2], there exists a s_{i} -rainbow circuit for (M_{j}^{\prime},c_{j}^{\prime}). 
Thus if for some i\in[3], C is an s_{i} -rainbow circuit for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) and D is an s_{i} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}), then C\cap F\neq\mathchar 31 and D\cap F^{\prime}\neq\mathchar 31 . For otherwise, C\triangle D is a rainbow cycle in (M,c). 




Case 1.2.1 Suppose M_{2}\big|F^{\prime}\cup S\not\simeq M(K_{4}). 




By Lemma 11, there exists j\in[3] for which there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit C for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |C\cap F^{\prime}|\leq 1 and |C|\leq n_{2}-1. We may assume that s_{1}\in C. Since C\cap F^{\prime}\neq\mathchar 31, we may assume that C\cap F^{\prime}=\{f_{1}\}. Suppose first that
 M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} is simple. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(f_{1}). Then (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is seen to be circuit-achromatic and by assumption Theorem 2 iii)
holds for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Thus there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . We may assume that s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime} , for if s_{1}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}, then \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCP for (M,c). 
Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle C we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). Suppose instead that M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1} is not simple. Then f_{1} and s_{1} are parallel. Let M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+f_{1} and let c_{2}^{\prime\prime} be a colouring
of M_{2}^{\prime\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f_{1}):=c(f_{1}), c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f_{3}^{\prime}):=c(f_{2}^{\prime}), and c_{2}^{\prime\prime}\big|E(M_{2}^{\prime})-f_{3}=c_{2}^{\prime}. Then M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is simple, (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic and \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{2}-2. Thus by assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) and S and
there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). Note that for i=1,2, D_{i}\cap\{f_{1},f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime},f_{3}^{\prime}\}\neq\mathchar 31. We may assume that s_{2}\in D_{2}. 




Case 1.2.1.1 f_{1} is the only element of F which is parallel with an element of S. 




Suppose f_{1}\in D_{2}. Then D_{2}^{\prime}=D_{2}\triangle S\triangle\{s_{1},f_{1}\}=D_{2}-f_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3} is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Thus D_{2}^{\prime} contains exactly one of f_{2}^{\prime} or f_{3}^{\prime} and we may assume f_{2}^{\prime}\in D_{2}^{\prime}. We can use C=D_{2}^{\prime} in the previous argument, noting that by assumption,
 f_{2} is not parallel with s_{3}. Thus we may assume that f_{1}\not\in D_{2}. If D_{2} contains exactly one of the elements f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime},f_{3}^{\prime}, then we could use C=D_{2} in the previous argument (as no element of F is parallel with s_{2} ).
Thus we may assume that \{f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime}\}\subset D_{2}. Let M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{2} and let c_{1}^{\prime\prime} be a colouring of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}):=c(f_{2}) , c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(f_{3}):=c(f_{1}) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big|E(M_{1}^{\prime})-f_{3}=c_{1}^{\prime}. Then M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit D . Then s_{2}\in D . If f_{1}\not\in D, then D\triangle D_{2} is a rainbow cycle of (M,c). Thus f_{1}\in D and hence f_{3}\not\in D. Now D^{\prime}=D\triangle S\triangle\{s_{1},f_{1}\}=D-f_{1}-s_{1}+s_{3} is
an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) . However, D^{\prime}\cap F=\mathchar 31, contradicting a previous observation. Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption, there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime}, for
otherwise, \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCP for (M,c). If f_{1}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}, then letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2} we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). On the other hand, if f_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime}, 
then C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle\{s_{1},f_{1}\}=C_{1}^{\prime}-f_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3} is a circuit where C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\cap F=\mathchar 31. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Then letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\triangle D we see that (C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}) is a T -SRCyP
for (M,c). 




Case 1.2.1.2 f_{2} or f_{3} is parallel with an element of S. 




We may assume that f_{2} and s_{2} are parallel. If f_{3} is parallel with s_{3} , then M_{2}+F is simple, has 2n_{2} elements and hence has a rainbow circuit. Thus f_{3} is not parallel with s_{3}. 
Consequently, M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{3} is simple. Define a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} for M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where
 c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{3})=c(f_{3}), c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(f_{2}):=c(f_{1}) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big|E(M_{1}^{\prime})-f_{2}=c_{1}^{\prime}. Suppose (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit D . Then s_{3}\in D and hence f_{3}\not\in D. 
Since D is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) we have that D\cap F\neq\mathchar 31. We may assume f_{1}\in D (and f_{2}\not\in D ). Then D^{\prime}=D\triangle S\triangle\{s_{1},f_{1}\}=D-s_{3}-f_{1}+s_{2} is seen to be an s_{2} -rainbow circuit for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) for which
 D^{\prime}\cap F=\mathchar 31, a contradiction. Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption, there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . We may assume that s_{3}\in C_{1}^{\prime} ; otherwise, \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCP for (M,c). Suppose that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap F\neq\mathchar 31. Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D where D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) for which |D|\leq n_{2}. Then we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). Suppose instead that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap F\neq\mathchar 31. We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap F=\{f_{1}\}. Let C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle\{s_{1},f_{1}\}=C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{3}-f_{1}+s_{2} and let D be an s_{2} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\triangle D we seen that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 1.2.2 Suppose M_{2}\big|F^{\prime}\cup S\simeq M(K_{4}.). 




In this case, let M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+F^{\prime}. Then r(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})=n_{1}+1 and \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})=\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+3=2n_{1}=2r(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})-2. We note that \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})<\varepsilon(M) since \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}>3. Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where for i=1,2,3 c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(f_{i}^{\prime}):=c(f_{i}). Then M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple and (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. By assumption there is a T -SRCP for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) and such is seen to be a T -SRCP for (M,c). 




Case 2 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-4. 




We have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-2 and |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|\geq n_{2}-1. 
By assumption, Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S . Thus there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where we may assume that for i=1,2, s_{i}\in D_{i}. 
Let M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2}. 




Case 2.1 |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}-1. 




We have that (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has no colour-singular elements and the same holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where for i=1,2, c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i}):=0. 
It is straightforward to show that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic and hence M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is also simple. Since \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{1}-2, it follows by assumption that there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Now using similar arguments as before, one can construct a T -SRCP \{C_{1},C_{2}\} for (M,c) using the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i},\ i=1,2. 




Case 2.2 |c_{2}^{\prime}(M_{2}^{\prime})|=n_{2}. 




We have that (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) contains two colour-singular elements which we denote by f_{i},\ i=1,2 . Then f_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2 are the colour-singular elements of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). We extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} to M_{1}^{\prime\prime} where




 (c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}),c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(c(f_{1}),c(f_{2}))&\mathrm{if}\ f_{1}\in D_{1}\ \mathrm{or}\ f_{2}\in D_{2}\\
(c(f_{2}),c(f_{1}))&\mathrm{otherwise}\end{array}.\right. 



Suppose first that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is simple. Then by assumption, there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). If for i=1,2, \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\not\subset C_{i}^{\prime}, then similar to before, one can construct a T -SRCP for (M,c) using the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i},\ i=1,2. We may assume that \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C_{1}^{\prime}. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{2}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Suppose instead that M_{1}^{\prime\prime} is not simple. Then for some x\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) and some i\in\{1,2\}, x is parallel with s_{i}. We may assume that x is parallel with s_{1} . Clearly x\in\{f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime}\} , for otherwise, D_{1}-s_{1}+x is a rainbow circuit of (M,c). 
We may assume that x=f_{1}^{\prime}. Now f_{2}^{\prime} is not parallel with s_{2} or s_{3} , for otherwise M_{2}^{\prime}+f_{1}^{\prime}+f_{2}^{\prime} would be a simple matroid with 2n_{2} elements and hence would contain a rainbow circuit. Let M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+f_{1}^{\prime} and extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2}^{\prime\prime} of M_{2}^{\prime\prime} where c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(f_{1}^{\prime}):=c(f_{1}^{\prime}). Then \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{2}-1 and f_{2} is the unique colour-singular element for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). 
By assumption, Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). Thus there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) where s_{2}\in D_{1}^{\prime} and s_{3}\in D_{2}^{\prime}. Redefine (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) where M_{1}^{\prime\prime}=M_{1}^{\prime}+s_{2}+s_{3} and extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1}^{\prime\prime} of M_{1}^{\prime\prime} as follows:
Suppose that some i , f_{2}\in D_{i}^{\prime}. Then define c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i+1}):=c(f_{2}) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{4-i})=c(f_{1}). If f_{2}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime} and for some i , f_{1}\in D_{i}^{\prime} , then define
 c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i+1})=c(f_{1}) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{4-i})=c(f_{2}). Otherwise, if for i=1,2, f_{i}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}, then define c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2}):=c(f_{1}) and c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{3}):=c(f_{2}). We claim that (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic.
For suppose (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) has a rainbow circuit C . Then C\cap\{s_{2},s_{3}\}\neq\mathchar 31 and we may assume that s_{2}\in C. If s_{3}\in C , then C^{\prime}=C\triangle S=C-s_{2}-s_{3}+s_{1} is seen to be an s_{1} -rainbow circuit where C^{\prime}\cap\{f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime}\}=\mathchar 31. Thus for an s_{1} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}), it is seen that C^{\prime}\triangle D is a rainbow cycle of (M,c). Thus s_{3}\not\in C. We also have that C\cap\{f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime}\}\neq\mathchar 31 for otherwise (M,c) has a rainbow circuit. Suppose f_{2}^{\prime}\in C. Then c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})=c(f_{1}) 
and thus f_{1}^{\prime}\not\in C. By the definition of c_{1}^{\prime\prime}, we have that f_{2}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime} and thus it is seen that C\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} is a rainbow cycle of (M,c). Thus f_{2}^{\prime}\not\in C and hence f_{1}^{\prime}\in C. Then C^{\prime}=C\triangle\{f_{1}^{\prime},s_{1}\}\triangle S=C-s_{2}-f_{1}^{\prime}+s_{3} is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Then C^{\prime}\triangle D is seen to be rainbow circuit of (M,c). This proves our claim. Thus (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}) is simple and circuit-achromatic and \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})=2n_{1}-2. By assumption, there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime}). We may assume that \{s_{2},s_{3}\}\cap(C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime})\neq\mathchar 31 
for otherwise \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCP for (M,c). We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. 




Case 2.1.1.1 s_{3}\not\in C_{2}^{\prime}. 




Suppose first that s_{3}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. If f_{1}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime} , then letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} it is seen that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). Suppose f_{1}^{\prime}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Then c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})=c(f_{2}) and f_{2}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. 
We see that C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle\{f_{1}^{\prime},s_{1}\}\triangle S=C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{2}-f_{1}^{\prime}+s_{3} is a circuit. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\triangle D. Then \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 
Suppose s_{3}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Then for i=1,2, f_{i}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Let C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S=C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{2}-s_{3}+s_{1} and let D be an s_{1} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\triangle D , we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime}\} is a
 T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 2.1.1.2 s_{3}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. 




We may assume that f_{1}^{\prime}\in C_{1}^{\prime} , for if f_{1}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}, then one can construct (using similar arguments as before) a T -SRCyP for (M,c) using the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},D_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2. 
Thus we have that c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{2})=c(f_{2}) and f_{2}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Furthermore, by the definition of c_{1}^{\prime\prime}, it follows that f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime} and f_{2}\not\in D_{2}^{\prime}. Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2 , we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 3 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-5. 




We have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-1 and (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) contains a unique colour-singular element which we denote by f. We also have that f^{\prime} is the unique colour-singular element for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). We observe that there is no element of M_{1}^{\prime} which is parallel to an element of S , for if there was such an element x , then M_{2}^{\prime}+x would have 2n_{2} elements and hence would contain a rainbow circuit. Thus M_{1} is simple. We shall extend the colouring c_{1}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{1} of M_{1} in the following manner. By assumption, Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds
for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S . If i.1) holds and there exists an S -SRCT \{D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where for i=1,2,3, s_{i}\in D_{i}, then we may assume that f\not\in D_{2}\cup D_{3}. 
Define c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}):=c(f) and for i=2,3, define c_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i}):=0. 




We claim that (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic. For suppose it contains a rainbow circuit C . Then C\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. Suppose |C\cap S|=2. We may assume that
 \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C. We have that f^{\prime}\not\in C and C^{\prime}=C\triangle S=C-s_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3} is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit. Let D be an s_{3} -rainbow circuit for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Then C^{\prime}\triangle D is seen to be a rainbow cycle of (M,c), a contradiction. Suppose |C\cap S|=1. Suppose s_{1}\in C. Then f^{\prime}\not\in C and for an s_{1} -rainbow circuit D of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) , we have that C\triangle D is a rainbow cycle in (M,c). Thus we may assume that s_{2}\in C. Now regardless of whether Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) and S , there is an S -SRCP \{D_{2},D_{3}\} for which
 s_{i}\in D_{i},\ i=2,3 and f\not\in D_{2}\cup D_{3}. Now it is seen that C\triangle D_{2} is a rainbow cycle in (M,c), a contradiction. Thus (M_{1},c_{1}) is circuit-achromatic.




Since \varepsilon(M_{1})=2n_{1}-2, it follows by assumption that Theorem 2 iii) holds for (M_{1},c_{1}) and T. Thus there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1},c_{1}). Again, we may assume that for some i , C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31, and this we may assume is true for i=1. 
If C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31, then one can use similar arguments as in the previous cases to construct a T -SRCP for (M,c). Thus we may assume that C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




Case 3.1 s_{1}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. 




We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime} and s_{3}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. As before, there is an S -SRCP \{D_{2},D_{3}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where s_{i}\in D_{i}^{\prime},\ i=2,3 and f\not\in D_{2}\cup D_{3}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3}, we see that
 \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 




Case 3.2 s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. 




We may assume that s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Suppose first that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{1}^{\prime} and s_{3}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. Then f^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}. Let C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S=C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{1}-s_{2}+s_{3}. By assumption, Theorem 2 ii) holds
for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) . We may assume that f and s_{3} are not parallel; if they are, then we can assume that f\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) and revert back to Case 2. It follows that there is an s_{3} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) where
we may assume that f\not\in D_{2}^{\prime}. Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} and let C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime}. Then C_{i},\ i=1,2 are rainbow cycles in (M,c) and




 \displaystyle|C_{1}|+|C_{2}| 
 \displaystyle=|C_{1}^{\prime\prime}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}|-4 




 \displaystyle=|C_{1}^{\prime}|-1+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}|-4 




 \displaystyle\leq n_{1}+2-1+n_{2}+3-4=n+2. 



Thus \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\} and C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. Regardless of whether Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}), there is a S -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} where
for i=1,2, s_{i}\in D_{i} and f\not\in D_{2}. Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i},\ i=1,2 we see that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a T -SRCyP for (M,c). 





12 Proof of main theorem




In this section, we shall complete the proof of Theorem 1.
To begin with, we shall use the following simple observations:









12.1 Observation




Let (M,c) be a coloured binary matroid where \varepsilon(M)=2(r(M)+1) , r(M)\geq 3 and c is 2 -uniform. Suppose there are at least three colour classes where the elements are non-parallel.
If M contains a rainbow 2 -circuit, then it contains a SRCP.



Proof.



Suppose M contains a rainbow 2 -circuit C=\{e,f\}. Let M^{\prime}=M-C. We shall show that M^{\prime} has a rainbow circuit of size at most r(M). We have |c(M^{\prime})|=|c(M)|=r(M)+1\geq r(M^{\prime})+1 and \varepsilon(M^{\prime})=2r(M)\geq 2r(M^{\prime})\geq r(M^{\prime})+2. Thus M^{\prime} contains a rainbow circuit D . If |D|\leq r(M), then D is the desired circuit. Suppose instead that |D|>r(M). Then |D|=r(M)+1 and c(D)=c(M). 
Since M contains at least 3 colour classes which are non-parallel, there exists x\in D which belongs to a colour class X=\{x,x^{\prime}\} for which x and x^{\prime} are non-parallel. Then x^{\prime} is a chord of D and D+x^{\prime} contains a D,x^{\prime} -chordal circuit D^{\prime} where x\not\in D^{\prime}, D^{\prime} is a rainbow circuit, and |D^{\prime}|<|D|=r(M)+1. Thus D^{\prime} is the desired circuit.




From the above, there is a rainbow circuit D in M^{\prime} for which |D^{\prime}|\leq r(M). Now \{C,D\} is seen to be a SRCP.
∎










12.2 Observation




Let (M,c) be a coloured binary matroid where \varepsilon(M)=2r(M) and c is 2 -bounded, with the exception of one colour class which is a 3 -circuit. Then M has a rainbow circuit.



Proof.



We have that |c(M)|\geq r(M). If |c(M)|\geq r(M)+1, then clearly (M,c) has a rainbow circuit. As such, we may assume that |c(M)|=r(M). Let B be a rainbow subset where |B|=r(M). If B is not a basis, then it contains a rainbow circuit. Thus we may assume that B is a basis. Let \{e,f,g\} be the colour class which is a 3 -circuit where
we may assume that e\in B. Now B+f contains a circuit C . If e\not\in C, then C is a rainbow circuit. If e\in C, then C^{\prime}=C\triangle\{e,f,g\}=C-e-f+g is rainbow circuit. In either case, M contains a rainbow circuit.
∎










12.3 Observation




Suppose (M,c) is a simple, regular coloured matroid where M=M_{1}\oplus_{3}M_{2} and c is a 2 -uniform (r(M)+1) -colouring. Then either M has a SRCP or for exactly one i\in[2] ,
 M_{i}\backslash E(M_{3-i}) contains a rainbow circuit.



Proof.



Assume that (M,c) has no SRCP. Let E(M_{1})\cap E(M_{2})=T=\{t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\}. For i=1,2, let n_{i}=r(M_{i}), M_{i}^{\prime}=M_{i}-T and \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=\varepsilon(M_{i}^{\prime}). For i=1,2, let c_{i}^{\prime}=c\big|E(M_{i}^{\prime}). 
We have \varepsilon(M)=2(r(M)+1)=2(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)=2n_{1}+2n_{2}-2=\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}. 
Suppose that for i=1,2, (M_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Then for i=1,2, \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{i}-1 and hence \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=2n_{i}-1. 
Let e be the unique colour-singular element for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Then e^{\prime} is the unique colour-singular element for (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). By Theorem 2 i), there is a T -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where e\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. 
We may assume that for i=1,2, t_{i}\in C_{i}^{\prime}. By Theorem 2 i) (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has a T -SRCP \{D_{1},D_{2}\} where for i=1,2, t_{i}\in D_{i}. Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i} is seen that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a SRCyP
for (M,c) (implying that (M,c) has a SRCP), contradicting our assumption. We may assume that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit, say C_{1} where we may choose C_{1} so that |C_{1}|\leq n_{1}. Suppose (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) also contains a rainbow circuit, say C_{2} . We may choose C_{2} 
so that |C_{2}|\neq n_{2}. Then |C_{1}|+|C_{2}|\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n+2 and hence \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a SRCP for (M,c), contradicting our assumption. Thus only (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a rainbow circuit.
∎





Suppose (M,c) is a simple, regular coloured matroid where c is a 2 -uniform (r(M)+1) -colouring. We shall assume that Theorem 1 is true for all regular matroids with rank less than r(M). By Theorem 4, we may assume that M is neither graphic nor cographic.
It follows that M is a either a 2 -sum or 3 -sum of regular matroids.









(12.1) We may assume that there is no proper subset A\subset E(M) where M^{\prime}=M\big|A is such that \varepsilon(M^{\prime})=2(r(M^{\prime})+1). 



Proof.



Suppose there is a subset A\subset E(M) where M^{\prime}=M\big|A is such that \varepsilon(M^{\prime})=2(r(M^{\prime})+1). By assumption, M^{\prime} has a SRC 4 -tuple and such is seen to be a SRC 4 -tuple for M. 
Thus we may assume that there is no such subset A. 
∎










(12.2) We may assume that M is a 3 -sum of regular matroids.



Proof.



Suppose that M is a 2 -sum of regular matroids M=M_{1}\oplus_{2}M_{2} where E(M_{1})\cap E(M_{2})=\{s\}. For i=1,2, let M_{i}^{\prime}=M_{i}\backslash E(M_{3-i}), c_{i}^{\prime}=c\big|E(M_{i}^{\prime}), n_{i}=r(M_{i}) and \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=\varepsilon(M_{i}^{\prime}). 
We have that \varepsilon(M)=2(r(M)+1)=2(n_{1}+n_{2})=\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}. 
It follows by Lemma 2 that we may assume that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Thus \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{1}-1 and \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}\geq 2n_{2}+1. By (12), \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}\leq 2n_{2}+1 and hence it follows that
 \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}+1 and \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}-1. By Observation 1, (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a unique colour-singular element, say f and f^{\prime} is seen to be the unique colour-singular element of (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). 
If there is an element x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s , then we can add x to M_{1}^{\prime} , in which case M_{1}^{\prime} has 2n_{1} elements and M_{2}^{\prime} has 2n_{2} elements. Then for i=1,2, M_{i}^{\prime} contains a rainbow circuit
 C_{i} where |C_{i}|\leq n_{i} and \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is seen to be a SRCP for (M,c). Thus we may assume that no element of E(M_{2}^{\prime}) is parallel with s and hence M_{2} is simple. Extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2} of M_{2} where c_{2}(s)=c(f). Then c_{2} is a 2 -uniform (n_{2}+1) -colouring of M_{2} .
By assumption, there is a SRC 4 -tuple \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} for (M_{2},c_{2}). By Observation 1.1, there is an s -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq n_{1}. For all i\in[4], let
 C_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D&\mathrm{if}\ s\in C_{i}^{\prime}\\
C_{i}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ s\not\in C_{i}^{\prime}.\end{array}\right. Then it is seen that \sum_{i=1}^{4}|C_{i}|\leq 2n_{2}+4+2(|D|-2)\leq 2(n_{1}+n_{2})=2r(M)+2. Thus \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is a SRC 4 -tuple for (M,c). 
It follows from the above that we may assume that M is a 3 -sum of two regular matroids.
∎





By (12), we may assume that M is a 3 -sum M=M_{1}\oplus_{3}M_{2} of regular matroids where S=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\}=E(M_{1})\cap E(M_{2}) is co-simple. Furthermore, we may assume that for i=1,2, r(M_{i})\geq 4. For i=1,2, let M_{i}^{\prime}=M_{i}\backslash E(M_{3-i}), \varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}=|E(M_{i}^{\prime})|, and let c_{i}^{\prime}=c\big|E(M_{i}^{\prime}). 
By Lemma 2, we may assume that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic.




Let F (resp. F^{\prime} ) be the set of singular elements of (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) (resp. (M_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) ) If F\neq\mathchar 31 (resp. F^{\prime}\neq\mathchar 31 ), let F=\{f_{1},\dots,f_{\alpha}\} (resp. F^{\prime}=\{f_{1}^{\prime},\dots,f_{\alpha}^{\prime}\} ).









(12.3) 2r(M_{1})-3\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}\leq 2r(M_{1})-1. 



Proof.



Since M_{1}^{\prime} is simple and (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic, it follows that \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}\leq 2r(M_{1})-1. Also, by our assumptions, \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}\leq 2r(M_{2})+1. 
Since




 \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=\varepsilon(M)=2(r(M)+1)=2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2})-1), 



it follows that




 \displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime} 
 \displaystyle=2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2})-1)-\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime} 




 \displaystyle\geq 2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2})-1)-2r(M_{2})-1=2r(M_{1})-3. 



∎










(12.4) Suppose \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2r(M_{1})-1. Then F=\{f_{1}\} and there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}. Furthermore, we may assume that if (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a S -SRCT, then there is circuit D_{3}^{\prime} such that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is an S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 



Proof.



By Observation 1, we have that |F|=1 and hence F=\{f_{1}\}. 
We have that Theorem 2 i.1) or i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). If i.1) holds, then there is an S -SRCT \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} and hence for some i<j, f_{1}\not\in D_{i}^{\prime}\cup D_{j}^{\prime}. If Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}), then there is a S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}. Thus regardless of whether i.1) or i.2) holds, there is an S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}. Furthermore, in the case where (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a S -SRCT, we may assume that there is a circuit D_{3}^{\prime} such that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is an S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
∎










(12.5) Suppose that \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2r(M_{1})-1. Then (M,c) has a SRC 4 -tuple.



Proof.



By (12), F=\{f_{1}\} 
and there is a S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}. Furthermore, we may assume that if (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a S -SRCT, then there is circuit D_{3}^{\prime} such that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is an S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
Without loss of generality, we may assume that for i=1,2 s_{i}\in D_{i}^{\prime}. Note that F^{\prime}=\{f_{1}^{\prime}\} and f_{1} is not parallel with s_{1} or s_{2}. 
Extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2} of M_{2} where for i=1,2 , c_{2}(s_{i}):=0 and c_{2}(s_{3}):=c(f_{1}). Then c_{2} is a 2 -uniform (r(M_{2})+1) -colouring of M_{2}. Suppose M_{2} is not simple. Then there exists an element x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) and j\in[3] for which x is parallel with s_{j}. If \{x,s_{j}\} is a rainbow 2 -circuit, then it follows by Observation 12 that M_{2}^{\prime}+s_{j} has a SRCP (and hence also a SRC 4 -tuple). Suppose c_{2}(x)=c_{2}(s_{j}) . Then j=3, c_{2}(x)=c_{2}(s_{3})=c(f_{1}) and hence x=f_{1}^{\prime}. Now we may assume that f_{1}^{\prime}\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) and \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2n_{1}, \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2n_{2}-2 and F=\mathchar 31. We may now argue as in the proof of (12), where F=\mathchar 31, with the roles of M_{1} and M_{2} reversed. Thus we may assume that M_{2} is simple.




By assumption, (M_{2},c_{2}) has a SRC 4 -tuple \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} . We may assume that \left(\bigcup_{i}C_{i}^{\prime}\right)\cap S\neq\mathchar 31, for otherwise \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} a SRC 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 1 For exactly one i\in[4], C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31 and for all i=2,3,4, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31. Furthermore, we may assume that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=1, since if |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2, then C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S is a rainbow circuit where |C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\cap S|=1. 
Let C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i}\} . If i\in\{1,2\}, then C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime} is seen to be a rainbow cycle of M and |C_{1}|=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{i}^{\prime}|-2. Then




 \displaystyle|C_{1}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}| 
 \displaystyle=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{i}^{\prime}|-2+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}| 




 \displaystyle\leq 2r(M_{2})+4+r(M_{1})-2 




 \displaystyle=r(M_{2})+(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2}))+2 




 \displaystyle=r(M_{2})+r(M)+4\leq 2r(M)+4. 



Thus \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple in (M,c). Suppose instead that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{3}\}. Since |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}) , there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq r(M_{1}). 
Given that c_{2}(s_{3})=c(f_{1}), it follows that C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D is a rainbow cycle. Arguing as above D in place of D_{i}^{\prime} , one sees that \{C_{1},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 2 For exactly two integers i\in[4], C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




We may assume that for i=1,2, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31 and for i=3,4, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31. We may also assume that for i=1,2, |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=1 , for if for some i , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2, then we can use C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle S in place of C_{i}^{\prime}. 
Suppose first that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S\neq C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S. Then we may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\}. Suppose that C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. For i=1,2, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime} . Then
 C_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2 are seen to be rainbow cycles of (M,c) and |C_{1}|+|C_{2}|=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}|-4 . Thus we have




 \displaystyle|C_{1}|+|C_{2}|+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}|
