 \displaystyle=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}|-4+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}| 




 \displaystyle\leq 2r(M_{2})+4-4+r(M_{1})+2\leq 2r(M)+4. 



Suppose that C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{3}\}. If (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has an S -SRCT, then by assumption there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D_{3}^{\prime} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}), such that \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is an S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3}^{\prime}, we see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has no S -SRCT. Then there exists an S -SRCP
 \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where s_{1}\in D_{1}^{\prime\prime}, s_{3}\in D_{2}^{\prime\prime}, and f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime\prime}. Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime}, we see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Suppose now that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S. Assume first that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{1}\}. For i=1,2, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime}. Then |C_{1}|+|C_{2}|=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+2|D_{1}^{\prime}|-4 and thus




 \displaystyle|C_{1}|+|C_{2}|+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}| 
 \displaystyle=|C_{1}^{\prime}|+|C_{2}^{\prime}|+2|D_{1}^{\prime}|-4+|C_{3}^{\prime}|+|C_{4}^{\prime}| 




 \displaystyle\leq 2r(M_{2})+4-4+2r(M_{1}) 




 \displaystyle=2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2}))=2r(M)+4. 



Thus \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). If C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{2}\}, then similar reasoning can be used with D_{2}^{\prime} in place of D_{1}^{\prime}. 
Assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{3}\}. Since |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}), there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D|\leq r(M_{1}). Noting that C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D,\ i=1,2 are rainbow cycles, we see that as before that
 \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 3 For exactly three integers i\in[4], C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




We may assume that for all i\in[3], C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31 and C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\mathchar 31. 
We shall look at two sub-cases, depending on whether (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a S -SRCT or not.




Case 3.1 Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has an S -SRCT.




By assumption, there is a rainbow circuit D_{3}^{\prime} where \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is a S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
There are two sub-cases, depending on whether the sets C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S,i\in[3] are distinct or not.




Case 3.1.1 Suppose that the sets C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S,i\in[3] are not distinct.




We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S. Suppose first that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=1 and assume that for some i\in[3], C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{i}\}. Suppose i\in\{1,2\}. Then given that f_{1} is not parallel with s_{1} or s_{2}, there is a s_{i} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime\prime}. 
Define C_{i},\ i=1,2,3 as follows:




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{\prime})&\mathrm{if}\ C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}\\
(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime})&\mathrm{if}\ C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=S-s_{i}\end{array}\right. 



Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). Lastly, if C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{3}\}, then f_{1} is not parallel with s_{3} (otherwise, C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{3}+f_{1} is a rainbow circuit) and we can argue as before.




Suppose that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. Then for some i\in[2], C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i},s_{3}\}. Let S-\{s_{i},s_{3}\}=\{s_{j}\}. Then C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}. Given that f_{1} is not parallel with s_{j}, there is a s_{j} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime\prime}. Now define (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime}). Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple.




Case 3.1.2 Suppose that the sets C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S,\ i\in[3] are distinct.




For i=1,2,3 let C_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}\\
C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{j}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ S-C_{i}^{\prime}=\{s_{j}\}.\end{array}\right. We see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 3.2 Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has no S -SRCT.




We have that Theorem 2 i.2) holds for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) and S. Thus for all i,j,\ i<j, there exists an S -SRCP \{D_{i}^{ij},D_{j}^{ij}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where s_{i}\in D_{i}^{ij}, s_{j}\in D_{j}^{ij}, and f_{1}\not\in D_{i}^{ij}\cup D_{j}^{ij}. 
Again, we have two sub-cases, depending on whether the sets C_{i}\cap S,\ i\in[3] are distinct or not.




Case 3.2.1 Suppose that the sets C_{i}\cap S,\ i\in[3] are not distinct.




We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S. Suppose first that for some i\in[3], C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}=\{s_{i}\}. If i\in\{1,2\}, then f_{1} is not parallel with s_{i} and hence there is a s_{i} - semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{1}^{\prime\prime}. Define C_{i},\ i=1,2,3 as follows:




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{ij},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{ij},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{ij})&\mathrm{if}\ C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}\\
(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime})&\mathrm{if}\ C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=S-s_{i}.\end{array}\right. 



We see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple.




Suppose instead that for some i\in[2], C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=S-s_{i}. Then
 C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i}\}. As before, there exists a s_{i} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Define C_{i},\ i=1,2,3 such that
 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{2}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime}) . Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 3.2.2 Suppose that the sets C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S,\ i\in[3] are distinct.




Assume first that at least two of the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},\ i\in[3] contain only one element of S. We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i}\} and C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{j}\}. Let \{k\}=[3]-\{i,j\}. Define
 C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{i,j} and C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{ij} and let




 C_{3}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{k}^{kj}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{k}\}\\
C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{k}^{kj}&\mathrm{if}\ S-C_{3}^{\prime}=\{s_{k}\}\\
C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{i}^{ij}&\mathrm{if}\ S-C_{3}^{\prime}=\{s_{i}\}\\
C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{j}^{ij}&\mathrm{if}\ S-C_{3}^{\prime}=\{s_{j}\}\end{array}\right. 



We see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Assume that at least two (and hence exactly two) of the circuits C_{i}^{\prime},\ i\in[3] contain two elements of S. We may assume that for i=1,2, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i},s_{3}\} and C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{k}\}. For i=1,2, define C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{3-i}^{12} and define




 C_{3}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{k}^{12}&\mathrm{if}\ k\in[2]\\
C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3}^{13}&\mathrm{if}\ k=3.\end{array}\right. 



Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 4 Suppose that for all i\in[4], C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq\mathchar 31. 




We shall examine two sub-cases depending whether (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has a S -SRCT or not.




Case 4.1 Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has an S -SRCT.




By assumption, there is a rainbow circuit D_{3}^{\prime} where \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime}\} is an S -SRCT for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 




Case 4.1.1 Suppose that for all i\in[4] , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=1. 




For all i\in[4] , let C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i^{\prime}}\} and let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime} .
Then




 \displaystyle\sum_{i}|C_{i}| 
 \displaystyle=\sum_{i}|C_{i}^{\prime}|+\sum_{i}|D_{i^{\prime}}|-8 




 \displaystyle\leq 2(r(M_{2}))+4+2(r(M_{1})+2)-8 




 \displaystyle=2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2}))=2r(M)+4. 



Thus \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be an SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 4.1.2 Suppose that for some i\in[4] , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. 




We may assume that |C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S|=2 and C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. Since each element s_{i} belongs to at most two of the circuits C_{j}^{\prime},\ j\in[4], it follows that for some j\in\{2,3,4\}, s_{2}\in C_{j}^{\prime}. We may assume that s_{2}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. Since f_{1} is not parallel with s_{2}, there is a s_{2} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{2}^{\prime\prime}. 




Suppose that for i=2,3,4 , |C_{i}^{\prime}\ \cap S|=1 and for i=2,3,4, let C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i^{\prime}}\}. Since S\subset C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}, we have 3^{\prime}\neq 4^{\prime}. Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime}, C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime} , and for i=3,4, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}. Then we see that
 \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). By the above, we may assume that for some i\in\{2,3,4\} , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. Suppose first that C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. We may assume this is true for i=3. 
Then for i=2,4, C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. For i=1,3, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime} and for i=2,4, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime}. Then




 \displaystyle\sum_{i}|C_{i}| 
 \displaystyle=\sum_{i}|C_{i}^{\prime}|+2(|D_{1}^{\prime\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime\prime}|)-10 




 \displaystyle\leq 2r(M_{2})+4+2(r(M_{1})+3)-10 




 \displaystyle=2(r(M_{1})+r(M_{2}))=2r(M)+4. 



Thus \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). Suppose instead that there is a circuit C_{i}^{\prime} where |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2 and C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S. We may assume this is true for i=2 and moreover, C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2},s_{3}\}, C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\} and C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. 
Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{\prime},\ C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} and C_{3}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime}\ C_{4}=C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime}. Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 4.2 Suppose that (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has no S -SRCT.




For all i,j,\ i<j, there exists an S -SRCP \{D_{i}^{ij},D_{j}^{ij}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where s_{i}\in D_{i}^{ij}, s_{j}\in D_{j}^{ij}, and f_{1}\not\in D_{i}^{ij}\cup D_{j}^{ij}. 




Case 4.2.1 Suppose that for all i\in[4] , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=1. 




For all i\in[4] , let C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i^{\prime}}\}. 
Then for some i<j , we have that s_{i^{\prime}}=s_{j^{\prime}}. We may assume that s_{1^{\prime}}=s_{2^{\prime}}=s_{1}. If s_{3^{\prime}}=s_{4^{\prime}}=s_{j}, then define (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{1j},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{1j},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{1j},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{j}^{1j}). 
Otherwise, if s_{3^{\prime}}\neq s_{4^{\prime}}, then define (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{13^{\prime}},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{14^{\prime}},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3^{\prime}}^{13^{\prime}},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{4^{\prime}}^{14^{\prime}}). In either case, we see that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 4.2.2 Suppose that for some i\in[4] , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. 




We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. Then for some i\in\{2,3,4\} s_{2}\in C_{i}^{\prime} and we may assume this is true for i=2. Suppose that for i=2,3,4, |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=1. 
Then C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\} and for some i\in\{3,4\} , C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\} or \{s_{3}\}. We may assume that C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\}. Then C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\} or \{s_{3}\}. Given that f_{1} and s_{2} are non-parallel, there is a s_{2} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{2}^{\prime\prime}. 
Define (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}) as follows:
If C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}, then let




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{12},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{12}). 



Otherwise, if C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{3}\}, then define




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{12},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{23},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{12},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3}^{23}). 



In either case, \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Lastly, suppose that for some i\in\{2,3,4\}, |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S|=2. Suppose that C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\}. We may assume that C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1},s_{3}\} and C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. 
As before, there is a s_{2} -semi-SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime\prime},D_{2}^{\prime\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where f_{1}\not\in D_{2}^{\prime\prime}. Let




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{\prime\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime\prime}). 



Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). Suppose now that C_{i}^{\prime}\cap S\neq C_{1}^{\prime}\cap S. Then we may assume that C_{2}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2},s_{3}\} and C_{3}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{1}\} and C_{4}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{2}\}. 
Let




 (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4})=(C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{2}^{12},C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D_{1}^{12},C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{12},C_{4}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{12}). 



Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 
∎










(12.6) Suppose that \varepsilon(M_{1})=2r(M_{1})-2. Then (M,c) has a SRC 4 -tuple.



Proof.



Since \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime})=2r(M_{1})-2, we have \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime})=2r(M_{2}) and r(M_{1}^{\prime})-1\leq|c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|\leq r(M_{1}^{\prime}). If |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}^{\prime})-1 , then |F|=0; otherwise if |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}^{\prime}), then |F|=2. 
By Theorem 2 ii) there is a S -SRCP \{D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime}\} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). We may assume that for i=1,2 , let D_{i}^{\prime}\cap S=\{s_{i}\}. Observe that if |F|=2, then F=\{f_{1},f_{2}\} and for i=1,2, F\cap D_{i}^{\prime}\neq\mathchar 31. In this case, we may assume that for i=1,2, 
 f_{i}\in D_{i}^{\prime}. 
Extend c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2}^{\prime\prime} of M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+s_{1}+s_{2} where for i=1,2 ,




 c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\mathrm{if}\ F=\mathchar 31\\
c(f_{i})&\mathrm{if}\ |F|=2.\end{array}\right. 



Then c_{2}^{\prime\prime} is a 2 -uniform (r(M)+1) -colouring of M_{2}^{\prime\prime}. 
Suppose that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) contains a rainbow circuit C. Then C\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. If for some i\in[2], s_{i}\in C and s_{3-i}\not\in C, then C\triangle D_{i}^{\prime} is a rainbow cycle in (M,c). Suppose \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C. Then |F|=2, |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}) and hence there is a s_{3} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). Now C\triangle S\triangle D is seen to be a rainbow cycle in (M,c) , a contradiction. It follows from the above that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is circuit-achromatic.









(A) We may assume that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRC 4 -tuple.



Proof.



If M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is simple, then it follows by our assumptions that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRC 4 -tuple. Suppose that M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is not simple. Then there exists x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) and i\in\{1,2\} such that x is parallel with s_{i}. Without loss of generality, we may assume that x is parallel with s_{1}. Suppose that c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x)\neq c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}). Then \{x,s_{1}\} is a rainbow 2-circuit and hence it follows by Observation 12 that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRCP (which implies that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRC 4 -tuple).
Thus we may assume that c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x)=c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}) and thus x=f_{1}^{\prime}. Now F\neq\mathchar 31, and hence |F|=|F^{\prime}|=2. If f_{2}^{\prime} is parallel with s_{2} , then we can assume that for i=1,2,\ f_{i}^{\prime}\in E(M_{1}^{\prime}) , in which case \varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=2r(M_{1}) and \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2r(M_{1})-2. Now we can switch the roles of M_{1} and M_{2} and argue as before where F=\mathchar 31. Thus we may assume that f_{2}^{\prime} and s_{2} are non-parallel and hence M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime} is simple.




Suppose that M_{1}^{\prime}+f_{1}^{\prime} has a rainbow circuit D . If M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime} has a rainbow circuit, then it follows by Observation 12, that (M,c) has a SRCP. Thus we may assume that (M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic. Seeing as M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime} is simple and \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime})=2n_{2}-1, Theorem 2 i) holds for (M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}). Suppose that (M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has an S -SRCT (C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime}) where for i=1,2, s_{i}\in C_{i}^{\prime}. 
If f_{2}^{\prime}\not\in C_{2}^{\prime}, then letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2 , one sees that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is a SRCyP for (M,c) . Suppose f_{2}^{\prime}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. Since |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|=r(M_{1}), there is an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D_{3}^{\prime} for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D_{3}^{\prime}|\leq r(M_{1}). 
Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}-s_{1}+f_{1}^{\prime} and C_{2}=C_{3}^{\prime}\triangle D_{3}^{\prime}. Then \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is seen to be a SRCyP for (M,c) . Suppose that (M_{2}^{\prime}-f_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}) has no S -SRCT. Then there is an S -SRCP \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime}\} where s_{i}\in C_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2 and f_{2}^{\prime}\not\in C_{1}^{\prime}\cup C_{2}^{\prime}. Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i}^{\prime},\ i=1,2, one sees that \{C_{1},C_{2}\} is SRCyP for (M,c). 




From the above, we may assume that that M_{1}^{\prime}+f_{1}^{\prime} is circuit-achromatic. However, we can redefine M_{i},\ i=1,2 so that f_{1}^{\prime}\in E(M_{1}) and f_{1}^{\prime}\not\in E(M_{2}) , in which case we have that \varepsilon(M_{1}^{\prime})=2r(M_{1})-1. It now follows by (12) that (M,c) has a SRC 4 -tuple.
∎





By (12), we may assume that \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is SRC 4 -tuple for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). We may also assume that \left(\bigcup_{i}C_{i}^{\prime}\right)\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. 




Case 1 Suppose there exist distinct i,j\in[4] for which C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=C_{j}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. 




We may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=C_{2}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31. Suppose that \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C_{1}^{\prime}. Then for i=1,2, c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{i})=c(f_{i}) and F=\{f_{1},f_{2}\}. As before, there exists an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
Letting C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D,\ i=1,2, we see that
 \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). Thus we may assume that C_{1}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\{s_{1}\}. For i=1,2, let C_{i}=C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{1}^{\prime} and for i=3,4 let C_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{2}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ s_{2}\in C_{i}^{\prime}\\
C_{i}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ s_{2}\not\in C_{i}^{\prime}.\end{array}\right. 
Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 




Case 2 Suppose that the nonempty sets among C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\},\ i\in[4], are distinct.




Suppose that for some i\in[4], \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C_{i}^{\prime}. We may assume that \{s_{1},s_{2}\}\subset C_{1}^{\prime}. Then for i=2,3,4 , |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}|\leq 1 and it follows that for some i\in[4], 
 C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\mathchar 31. We may assume that C_{4}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\mathchar 31. Furthermore, we may assume that for i=2,3 , if C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31, then s_{i-1}\in C_{i}^{\prime}. 
As in Case 1, there exists an s_{3} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}). 
Letting C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle S\triangle D and for i=2,3, 




 C_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i-1}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31\\
C_{i}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\mathchar 31\end{array}\right. 



one sees that \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}^{\prime}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 
Suppose instead that for all i\in[4], |C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}|\leq 1. For all i\in[4], if C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31 , then let C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\{s_{i^{\prime}}\}. For all i\in[4], let




 C_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{i}^{\prime}\triangle D_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\mathchar 31\\
C_{i}^{\prime}&\mathrm{if}\ C_{i}^{\prime}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\mathchar 31.\end{array}\right. 



Then \{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\} is a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 
∎










(12.7) Suppose that \varepsilon(M_{1})=2r(M_{1})-3. Then (M,c) has a SRC 4 -tuple.



Proof.



Since (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) is circuit-achromatic, we have |c_{1}^{\prime}(M_{1}^{\prime})|\leq r(M_{1}) and thus |F|=1 or |F|=3. We also have that \varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}=2r(M_{2})+1. 









(B) For some j\in[3], there is an s_{j} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D\cap F|\leq 1 and |D|\leq r(M_{1})-1. 



Proof.



Suppose that M_{1}\big|F\cup S\simeq M(K_{4}). Let M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+F. We see that r(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=r(M_{2})+1 and




 \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})=\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime}+3=2r(M_{2})+4=2(r(M_{2}^{\prime\prime})+1). 



This violates (12).
Thus M_{1}\big|F\cup S\not\simeq M(K_{4}) and it follows by Lemma 11 that for some j\in[3] , (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) has an s_{j} -rainbow circuit D for which |D\cap F|\leq 1 and |D|\leq r(M_{1})-1. 
∎





By (12), for some i\in[3] there is an s_{i} -rainbow circuit D for (M_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}) where |D\cap F|\leq 1 and |D|\leq r(M_{1})-1. Here we may assume this is true for i=1. 
Extend the colouring c_{2}^{\prime} to a colouring c_{2}^{\prime\prime} of M_{2}^{\prime\prime}=M_{2}^{\prime}+s_{1}, where




 c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}c(f_{i})&\mathrm{if}\ D\cap F=\{f_{i}\}\\
c(f_{1})&\mathrm{if}\ D\cap F=\mathchar 31\end{array}\right. 



We see that c_{2}^{\prime\prime} is a 2 -uniform (r(M_{2})+1) -colouring of M_{2}^{\prime\prime}. Moreover, (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) is seen to be circuit-achromatic.









(C) We may assume that there is a SRC 4 -tuple for (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). 



Proof.



If M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is simple, then it follows by assumption that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has SRC 4 -tuple. Suppose M_{2}^{\prime\prime} is not simple. Then there is an element x\in E(M_{2}^{\prime}) which is parallel with s_{1}. 
If c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x)\neq c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}), then \{x,s_{1}\} is a rainbow 2 -circuit of (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}). It follows by Observation 12 that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRCP. Thus we may assume that c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(x)=c_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s_{1}) and thus x\in F^{\prime}. 
Suppose that M_{1}^{\prime}+x contains a rainbow circuit D^{\prime}. Since \varepsilon(M_{2}^{\prime}-x)=2r(M_{2}) and M_{2}^{\prime}-x is simple, it follows by Corollary 3 that (M_{2}^{\prime}-x,c_{2}^{\prime}) has a rainbow circuit. It now follows by
Observation 12 that (M,c) has a SRCP. Thus we may assume that M_{1}^{\prime}+x is circuit-achromatic. However, replacing M_{1} by M_{1}+x and M_{2} by M_{2}-x, it follows by (12) that (M,c) has a SRC 4 -tuple.
∎





By (12), we may assume that (M_{2}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime}) has a SRC 4 -tuple \{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}\} and we may also assume that s_{1}\in C_{1}^{\prime}. 
If s_{1}\in C_{2}^{\prime}\cup C_{3}^{\prime}\cup C_{4}^{\prime}, then we may assume that s_{1}\in C_{2}^{\prime}. Let C_{1}=C_{1}^{\prime}\triangle D and let C_{2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{2}^{\prime}\triangle D&\mathrm{if}\ s_{1}\in C_{2}^{\prime}\\
C_{2}^{\prime}&\mathrm{otherwise.}\end{array}\right. 
Then (C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}^{\prime},C_{4}^{\prime}) is seen to be a SRCy 4 -tuple for (M,c). 
∎

Instructions for reporting errors
 
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
 support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
 methods listed below:
 
 Click the "Report Issue" (
 
 ) button, located in the page header.
 
 
Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
 
Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
 may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
 should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
 all.
 
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion, and welcome developer contributions.
